Per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0117/spec.md`; evidence under `evidence/T06.15/`.\n20-\n21----\n22-\n23-## D-1: PTY layer — fork ptytest vs build minimal pexpect wrapper\n24-\n25-**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n26-**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n27-**Roadmap cross-reference:** NFR-MAINT1 (roadmap row 23) — vendored ptytest fork under `cli/eval/pty/`; AC10 (row 25) — fork SHA pin + drift policy; COMP-007 (row 36) — PtyDriver consumes the vendored layer.\n28-\n29-### Context\n30-\n31-Real Claude Code is a TTY-based interactive REPL. The eval harness needs a PTY (pseudo-terminal) driver that can spawn it, write to its stdin, read from its stdout/stderr, detect \"prompt ready\" states, kill on timeout, and capture exit codes.\n32-\n33-### Options\n34-\n35-| Option | Pros | Cons | LOC | Risk |\n36-|---|---|---|---|---|\n37-| **A. Fork `brandon-fryslie/ptytest`** | Working Claude Code example already exists in repo. Plugin-free standalone class. Active enough (recent commits). | 1 star / 0 forks → bus-factor real. Includes pytest-fixture machinery we don't need. | ~300 LOC adaptation | LOW (ptytest is small, easy to own) |\n38-| B. Build minimal `pexpect` wrapper from scratch | Clean ownership, no upstream-drift risk, exactly the API we want. | More code to write, debug, test. We re-derive Claude Code TTY quirks. | ~700 LOC fresh | MEDIUM (pexpect has subtle gotchas) |\n39-| C. Use `pexpect` directly (no wrapper) | Zero abstraction overhead. | Every eval re-implements spawn/wait/expect_prompt patterns; code duplication. | ~0 LOC harness; +~50 LOC per eval | HIGH (drift across evals) |\n40-\n41-### Decision: **A — fork ptytest, vendor under `cli/eval/pty/`, fork-and-own posture.**\n42-\n43-### Rationale\n44-\n45-1. The fork-research report verified ptytest has a working Claude Code example, which means the hardest TTY edge cases (prompt detection, ANSI escape handling, line-buffered reads) are already solved.\n46-2. Bus-factor risk is mitigated by **vendoring** (we copy the code in; no runtime pip dependency on the upstream repo). If upstream dies, we have a frozen working copy.\n47-3. The durable dependency is `pexpect` (2.8k stars, active again at v4.9) — ptytest is just a thin layer on top.\n48-4. 300 LOC adaptation is ~5x cheaper than 700 LOC fresh build.\n49-5. Option C (raw pexpect) fails the harness-cleanliness goal — evals should look like assertion code, not PTY plumbing.\n50-\n51-### Consequences\n52-\n53-- We own a small vendored fork forever. `PROVENANCE.md` documents the fork SHA and our diffs.\n54-- Quarterly review of upstream `pexpect` releases; resync if security-relevant.\n55-- A new file at `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` reproducing upstream MIT terms (license compliance).\n56-\n57----\n58-\n59-## D-2: Assertion DSL — adopt `Expect.*` port vs raw assertions\n60-\n61-**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n62-**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n63-**Roadmap cross-reference:** COMP-010 (roadmap row 12) — ExpectDSL interface in M1; FR-EXP1 (row 64) — Expect.* primitives in M4; COMP-010.1..COMP-010.6 (rows 65–70) — per-primitive contracts.\n--\n158-     callback: superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start\n159-   ```\n160:   The callback is invoked only when an eval's logic can't be expressed in YAML (E14, possibly E15).\n161-4. New suites (`fast.yaml`, `regression.yaml`, etc.) drop in as data files with no code changes — exactly the kind of low-friction extension we want.\n162-5. `loader.py` validates against `suites/suite.schema.json` once; the rest of the pipeline trusts validated input.\n163-\n164-### Consequences\n165-\n166-- `suites/suite.schema.json` is a public artifact (committed) — schema changes need versioning.\n167-- Callbacks live in `suites/<suite-name>_callbacks.py` co-located with the YAML.\n168-- Eval IDs (`E1`, `E2.1`, etc.) are stable identifiers — must not be renamed without a migration note.\n169-- Parameterized evals (e.g., E2's 3 prefix variants) use a `parameterize:` block in YAML; the loader expands these into separate `EvalSpec` instances at load time.\n170-\n171----\n172-\n173-## D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate — falsifiable definition of \"equivalent regression\"\n174-\n175-**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n176-**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n177-**Roadmap cross-reference:** FR-G5 (roadmap row 75) — falsifiable hook-matcher coverage gate CLI entry; TEST-013 (row 101) — coverage-gate tests; MIG-002 (row 103) — eval-batch rollout references coverage map.\n178-\n179-### Context\n180-\n181-Goal G5 in design-spec §1 said \"catch the bug PR #49 fixed and any equivalent future regression\" — but \"equivalent\" was undefined. Spec-panel finding W-1 (CRITICAL) flagged this as unfalsifiable: with no measurable acceptance criterion, the harness has no way to answer \"did I do my job?\"\n182-\n183-### Options\n184-\n185-| Option | Pros | Cons |\n186-|---|---|---|\n187-| **A. Coverage gate: every matcher P in `hooks.json` must have ≥1 eval in the loaded suite** | Falsifiable, mechanically enforceable. CI-friendly. Maps to the actual class of bugs PR #49 lived in (matcher↔case-body drift). | Requires `eval doctor --check-coverage`. New eval must accompany every new hook matcher. |\n188-| B. The durable dependency is `pexpect` (2.8k stars, active again at v4.9) — ptytest is just a thin layer on top.\n48-4. 300 LOC adaptation is ~5x cheaper than 700 LOC fresh build.\n49-5. Option C (raw pexpect) fails the harness-cleanliness goal — evals should look like assertion code, not PTY plumbing.\n50-\n51-### Consequences\n52-\n53-- We own a small vendored fork forever. `PROVENANCE.md` documents the fork SHA and our diffs.\n54-- Quarterly review of upstream `pexpect` releases; resync if security-relevant.\n55-- A new file at `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` reproducing upstream MIT terms (license compliance).\n56-\n57----\n58-\n59-## D-2: Assertion DSL — adopt `Expect.*` port vs raw assertions\n60-\n61-**signed_off_by:** RyanW\n62-**signed_off_date:** 2026-05-20\n63-**Roadmap cross-reference:** COMP-010 (roadmap row 12) — ExpectDSL interface in M1; FR-EXP1 (row 64) — Expect.* primitives in M4; COMP-010.1..COMP-010.6 (rows 65–70) — per-primitive contracts.\n--\n158-     callback: superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start\n159-   ```\n160:   The callback is invoked only when an eval's logic can't be expressed in YAML (E14, possibly E15).\n161-4. Purpose\n14\t\n15\tOQ-2 (roadmap.md:110) asks: *\"Concrete content of E3–E15 manifest entries.\"*\n16\tThe design-spec (§5) shows only E1 and E2 parameterize as exemplars; E3–E15 are deferred.\n17\t\n18\tThis spec freezes the **inputs, expects, and capability tags** for each of the 13 remaining bodies so that T05.07 … T05.21 may author them without further design churn.\n19\t\n20\t## 2. Design constraints\n21\t\n22\t1. **Coverage:** every hook event type in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` MUST be exercised by ≥1 eval among {E1, E2.1–3, E3 … E15}.\n23\t2. **Determinism:** every body MUST pass deterministically on a clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) — no time-of-day, no network, no shared mutable state. (3-run determinism is the per-task AC.)\n24\t3. **No OQ-8 dependency:** E3–E15 MUST NOT require `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`. The original design-spec note tying E3 to \"30-min freshness tests\" is **superseded**; freshness-staleness via time offset is deferred until OQ-8 closes (separate follow-up).\n25\t4. **Expect.* primitive only:** every body's assertions MUST be expressible via the v1 DSL (`file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) plus the YAML `callback:` escape (D-4) for E14.\n26\t5. **Capability gating:** evals requiring a live MCP server soft-skip under `--no-mcp` with `skip_reason` populated (FR-CAP1).\n27\t\n28\t## 3. Frozen body shapes\n48\t\n49\tThe body shape for each eval is `(inputs, expects, capability_tags)`. Capability tags map to FR-CAP1 gates; `[]` means no capability gate (always runs).\n50\t\n51\t### E3 — SessionStart unmatched (session-init) hook fires\n52\t\n53\t| Field | Value |\n54\t|---|---|\n55\t| **Inputs** | spawn a fresh claude session via `PtyDriver.spawn(home=isolated)`; wait for prompt-ready. |\n56\t| **Expects** | `file.exists(state/session-init.log)` (script writes its own log); `jsonl.contains_event(logs/session-events.jsonl, type=session_init)`; `exit_code.equals(0)` (session exits cleanly on `/quit`). |\n57\t| **Capability tags** | `[]` (no MCP; no network) |\n58\t| **Notes** | Verifies the matcher-less first-position SessionStart hook fires before the matcher=* hook. Determinism: every spawn writes a new session log. |\n59\t\n60\t### E4 — SessionStart matcher=* freshness hook fires\n61\t\n62\t| Field | Value |\n63\t|---|---|\n64\t| **Inputs** | spawn a fresh claude session; wait for prompt-ready; `/quit`. |\n65\t| **Expects** | `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start)`; `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=session_start) == 1`. |\n66\t| **Capability tags** | `[]` |\n67\t| **Notes** | Verifies the SECOND SessionStart hook (matcher=*) also fires. E3 covers position 0; E4 covers position 1. |\n68\t\n69\t### E5 — UserPromptSubmit freshness hook fires\n70\t\n71\t| Field | Value |\n72\t|---|---|\n73\t| **Inputs** | spawn session; `inject_prompt(\"echo test\")`; observe pre-prompt hook output. |\n74\t| **Expects** | `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=user_prompt)`; `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=user_prompt) >= 1` per injected prompt. |\n75\t| **Capability tags** | `[]` |\n76\t| **Notes** | Time-offset-free: simply asserts the hook fires once per prompt submission. OQ-8-dependent staleness test is a follow-up eval. |\n77\t\n78\t### E6 — PreToolUse Edit matcher fires\n79\t\n80\t| Field | Value |\n81\t|---|---|\n82\t| **Inputs** | spawn session; inject prompt that triggers a single `Edit` tool call on a scratch file. |\n83\t| **Expects** | `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=pre_edit, matcher=Edit)`; `file.exists(<scratch>/edited.txt)`. |\n84\t| **Capability tags** | `[]` |\n85\t| **Notes** | Asserts the Edit branch of the `Edit\\|Write\\|mcp__serena__*` matcher resolves. |\n86\t\n87\t### E7 — PreToolUse Write matcher fires\n88\t\n89\t| Field | Value |\n90\t|---|---|\n91\t| **Inputs** | spawn session; inject prompt triggering a single `Write` tool call to a scratch file. |\n92\t| **Expects** | `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=pre_edit, matcher=Write)`; `file.exists(<scratch>/written.txt)`. |\n93\t| **Capability tags** | `[]` |\n94\t| **Notes** | Asserts the Write branch of the PreToolUse matcher. |\n95\t\n96\t### E8 — PreToolUse serena matcher fires\n97\t\n98\t| Field | Value |\n99\t|---|---|\n100\t| **Inputs** | spawn session; inject prompt triggering `mcp__serena__replace_content` on a scratch file (or other `mcp__serena__*` variant in the matcher). |\n101\t| **Expects** | `jsonl.contains_event(logs/freshness.jsonl, type=pre_edit, matcher=mcp__serena__*)`; `file.exists(<scratch>/modified.txt)`. |\n102\t| **Capability tags** | `[mcp_server.serena]` — soft-skip under `--no-mcp` or if serena unreachable. |\n103\t| **Notes** | Completes PreToolUse matcher coverage (Edit, Write, serena). |\n104\t\n105\t### E9 — PostToolUse Read async hook fires\n106\t\n107\t| Field | Value |\n108\t|---|---|\n109\t| **Inputs** | spawn session; None of that exists today; per the\n    # established T05.07..T05.19 deferral posture, T05.20 lands the\n    # OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n    #   - **N=3 → degenerate N=1.** A single PTY session (`/quit`)\n    #     fires the SessionStart hook chain, creates the sticky-pending\n    #     directory, and emits one session_init event — the same\n    #     observable shape E3 / E4 pin for SINGLE-session SessionStart.\n    #     The N=3 concurrency aspect (no-shared-mutable-state across\n    #     parallel spawns) is fully deferred to the callback escape\n    #     hatch.\n    #   - **per-`<sid>` sticky file → directory existence proxy.**\n    #     Expect.file has no glob support and no `<sid>` template\n    #     substitution; the per-session file\n    #     `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` cannot be asserted\n    #     without knowing the session_id ahead of time. This is the EXIT CODE OF THE EVAL PROCESS (the PTY\n    #     teardown contract), not the timeout-reap status of the slow\n    #     hook script.
